Having setup the EFT factorization in the three regions, with the formulae in Eqs. (
32
,
43
,
52
), we next implement resummation of large logarithms by exploiting these equations.
The resummation in SCET is carried out via a UV renormalization in the EFT and a subsequent renormalization group evolution (RGE) of these matrix elements. The RGE evolves these matrix elements from their natural scales
\mu_{i}
where logarithms are minimized to a common final scale
\mu
and results in resummations of large logarithms of ratios of the
\mu_{i}
scales.
The resummed cross sections will then allow us to extract the moments
M_{1}^{\kappa}(m_{J}^{2})
and
M_{-1}^{\kappa\varocircle}(m_{J}^{2})
introduced in Eq. (
5
).

As we mentioned above in Sec.
1
we will carry out the resummation at NLL
′
accuracy, where in addition to the NLL resummation, we will include NLO fixed order corrections to the perturbative functions in the factorization formulae in Eqs. (
32
), (
43
) and (
52
).
In Tab.
1
we summarize the orders to which the cusp anomalous dimension
\Gamma^{\rm cusp}[\alpha_{s}]
, various non-cusp anomalous dimensions
\gamma_{i}[\alpha_{s}]
, and
\beta
function are needed to achieve LL, NLL and NLL
′
accuracy. We collect expressions for the evolution kernels, anomalous dimensions, and details of evolution in App.
A
.
In the intermediate
R_{g}
regime, the
R_{g}
dependence appears directly through the evolution scales, and there are no further complications.
However, in the large
R_{g}
regime we see from Eq. (
34
) that the
R_{g}
-dependence is only seen at one loop in the collinear-soft function through a fixed-order term.
Thus in the large
R_{g}
regime we must include additional
R_{g}
dependent fixed order terms as indicated in the next to last column of Tab.
1
. Similarly, in the small
R_{g}
regime, the measurement of the jet mass appears as a fixed order correction on top of the
R_{g}
measurement. As we can see from Eq. (
3.3
), the leading order (LO) distribution for
m_{J}>0
starts at one-loop. Thus, in our NLL
′
treatment we will include additional fixed order terms relevant for these regimes, as indicated by entries in the last two columns of Tab.
1
.

Here a generic function
{\cal F}
in the factorization theorem is assumed to have a fixed order expansion of the following form:

\displaystyle{\cal F}(x,\mu)=\sum_{m=0}\sum_{n=1}^{2m}a_{mn}^{\cal F}\Big(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\Big)^{m}L_{\cal F}^{n}\,,

(59)

where
L_{\cal F}
are logarithms (transformed to Laplace space,
m_{J}^{2}\to x
, see App.
A
for details) and the
a_{mn}^{\cal F}
are coefficients which may contain non-logarithmic dependence on the variables.
For the large
R_{g}
factorization formula, the collinear-soft function
S_{c}\bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\bigr]
receives a one-loop
R_{g}
dependent correction that does not involve logarithms of
\mu
, which we indicate by
\alpha_{s}a_{10}^{S_{c}}\big(R_{g}/\theta_{g}^{\star}\big)
. This one-loop term constitutes the LO distribution of
R_{g}
in this regime and depends on
R_{g}
through the dimensionless ratio
R_{g}/\theta_{g}^{\star}
, where
\theta_{g}^{\star}
was defined above in Eq. (
12
). At NLL
′
order one would additionally
add the
R_{g}
dependent
{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})
terms in the large
R_{g}
regime, in order to include the dependence on
R_{g}
to one higher order. In our analysis we only include the
{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})
terms indicated in Tab.
1
which are determined by lower order anomalous dimensions and the one-loop constant terms, which omits the currently unknown
{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})

R_{g}
-dependent term in
S_{c}^{\kappa}
(we will estimate the impact of this term in our numerical uncertainty analysis). Finally, as
R_{g}\rightarrow 0
, the ratio
R_{g}/\theta_{g}^{\star}
becomes a power correction leading to factorization in the intermediate regime, where the standard treatment of NLL
′
counting applies.

A similar reasoning applies to the regime of small
R_{g}
, where the collinear function
{\cal C}^{q}
defined in Eq. (
3.3
) receives one-loop, non-logarithmic corrections depending on
m_{J}
. As mentioned above, for
m_{J}>0
, this is the LO distribution in the small-
R_{g}
regime, which we denote by
\alpha_{s}a_{10}^{\cal C}(\theta_{c}^{2}/R_{g}^{2})
, with
\theta_{c}
defined above in Eq. (
12
). We include the additional
{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})
fixed-order terms in this regime at NLL
′
in the same multiplicative way, as summarized in the table. Thus we note that the treatment of terms is slightly different in the three regimes. We will further justify our specific approach through the smooth matching between regimes as well as parametrize the uncertainty due to the unknown non-logarithmic 2-loop pieces in Sec.
5
. Furthermore, we note that, up to NLL
′
accuracy, we do not need to consider
{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})
corrections to the
\mu_{i}
-dependent SCET functions parametrized by
{\cal F}
in Tab.
1
(such as the jet, collinear-soft and global-soft functions) as their inclusion with
{\cal O}(\alpha_{s})
LO distributions in the small or large
R_{g}
regimes will only enter at
{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{3})
.

\!\!\Gamma^{\rm cusp}\!\!

\!\!\gamma\!\!

\!\!\beta(\alpha_{s})\!\!

\alpha_{s}^{m}L_{\cal F}^{n\geq 0}

Large
R_{g}
additions

Small
R_{g}
additions

LL

\alpha_{s}

-

\alpha_{s}

-

+\alpha_{s}\,a_{10}^{S_{c}}\big(\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}}\big)

+\alpha_{s}\,a_{10}^{\cal C}\big(\frac{\theta_{c}^{2}}{R_{g}^{2}}\big)

NLL

\alpha_{s}^{2}

\alpha_{s}

\alpha_{s}^{2}

-

+\alpha_{s}\,a_{10}^{S_{c}}\big(\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}}\big)

+\alpha_{s}\,a_{10}^{\cal C}\big(\frac{\theta_{c}^{2}}{R_{g}^{2}}\big)

NLL
′

\alpha_{s}^{2}

\alpha_{s}

\alpha_{s}\,
\displaystyle{\sum_{j=0}^{2}}

\!\!\!a_{1j}^{\cal F}L_{\cal F}^{j}

+\alpha_{s}^{2}\,a_{10}^{S_{c}}\big(\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}}\big)\displaystyle{\sum_{j=0}^{2}}a_{1j}^{\cal F}L_{\cal F}^{j}

+\alpha_{s}^{2}\,a_{10}^{\cal C}\big(\frac{\theta_{c}^{2}}{R_{g}^{2}}\big)\displaystyle{\sum_{j=0}^{2}}a_{1j}^{\cal F}L_{\cal F}^{j}

Table 1
:
Highest order of the anomalous dimensions and fixed order terms that are needed for the given accuracy. The last two columns indicate additional fixed order terms that are added in the large
R_{g}
and small
R_{g}
regime respectively.

Lastly, we note that the double differential cross section is affected by non-global logarithms,
which require resummation for a complete treatment of the double differential cross section in all regions.
In Sec.
3
we have shown how the leading NGLs affect the factorization formulae for our cross section in each of the regimes, giving rise to the functions
\Xi_{G}^{q}
,
\Xi_{S}^{q}
, and
\Xi_{C}^{q}
.
First of all, since we are interested in the moments
C_{1}^{q}
and
C_{2}^{q}
, the NGLs related to
\Xi_{G}
in the normalization cancel out as described in Sec.
3.4
.
Next, recall that we will focus on the case where the approximation
R_{g}/2\ll 1
can be assumed.
For the choice of kinematic parameters that we explore in Sec.
6
we will find that the variables
t_{S}
and
t_{C}
in Eqs. (
44
) and (
53
) are at most 0.2 across the allowed range of
R_{g}
for
m_{J}^{2}
in the SDOE region.
As was shown in Ref.
Kang:2019prh
, the result for LL large-
N_{c}
resummed NGLs for
R_{g}/2\ll 1
can be well approximated by the two-loop fixed order term when written in terms of the variable
t
, for
t\lesssim 0.3
(see Fig. 4 in Ref.
Kang:2019prh
).
Hence, the two loop term will suffice for our purposes, and we do not need to carry out a Monte Carlo LL resummation of the NGLs in our analysis.

Below in sections
4.1
,
4.2
,
3.3
we therefore explain how to carry out the resummation of global logarithms associated to the scales appearing in the remaining functions in the factorization formulae.
In the following, we first discuss the simpler cases of resummation in the small and intermediate
R_{g}
regimes and set up the relevant notation. Then we turn to the large
R_{g}
region, where the interplay between the LO term depending on
R_{g}/\theta^{\star}_{g}
and RG evolution requires more complicated calculations with Laplace transforms. Finally, we describe the resummation for the boundary corrections relevant to the calculation of
C_{2}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})
, where we must account for the shifted soft drop condition in Eq. (
6
).

4.1
Small
R_{g}

Resummation takes on the simplest form in the regime of small
R_{g}
, where the factorization structure is purely multiplicative. As written in Eq. (
52
), each function depends on a common and arbitrary renormalization scale
\mu
. Any one specific choice of
\mu
induces in the fixed-order ingredients large logarithms of the ratio of widely separated scales (indicated by the function arguments), which can be minimized by choosing instead using a different scale
\mu_{i}
for each function. These scales are then connected to a common scale
\mu
via renormalization group (RG) running, which is how the resummation of large logarithms is carried out in SCET.

Including the appropriate resummation factors, as discussed in App.
A.1
, the RG-evolved cross section in the small
R_{g}
regime for
e^{+}e^{-}
collisions reads

\displaystyle\frac{d\Sigma(R_{g})}{d(\textrm{ln}\,m_{J}^{2})}=\frac{m_{J}^{2}}{\big(Q\frac{R_{g}}{2}\big)^{2}}\,N_{q}^{\rm evol}(Q,Q_{\rm cut},\beta,R,\mu;\mu_{N},\mu_{gs})\>e^{\big[K_{cs_{g}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{g}})+K_{c}(\mu,\mu_{c})\big]}

(60)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\frac{\mu_{cs_{g}}}{Q_{\rm cut}(\frac{R_{g}}{2})^{1+\beta}}\Big)^{\omega_{cs_{g}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{g}})}\Big(\frac{\mu_{c}}{Q\frac{R_{g}}{2}}\Big)^{\omega_{c}(\mu,\mu_{c})}S_{c_{g}}^{q}\!\Big[\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\mu_{cs_{g}}\Big]{\cal C}^{q}\bigg[\frac{m_{J}^{2}}{Q^{2}R_{g}^{2}},QR_{g},\mu\bigg]\Xi_{C}^{q}(t_{C})\,,

where the dimensionless prefactor
N_{q}^{\rm evol}
is given by

\displaystyle N_{q}^{\rm evol}(Q,Q_{\rm cut},

\displaystyle\beta,R,\mu;\mu_{N},\mu_{gs})\equiv N^{q}(Q,R,\mu_{Q})\,S_{G}^{q}\Big(Q_{\rm cut}\tan^{1+\beta}\frac{R}{2},R,\beta,\mu_{gs}\Big)\,\Xi_{G}^{q}(t_{G})

(61)

\displaystyle\qquad\times e^{K_{N^{q}}(\mu,\mu_{N})+K_{gs}(\mu,\mu_{gs})}\Big(\frac{\mu_{N}}{Q\tan\frac{R}{2}}\Big)^{\omega_{N^{q}}(\mu,\mu_{N})}\Big(\frac{\mu_{gs}}{Q_{\rm cut}\tan^{1+\beta}\frac{R}{2}}\Big)^{\omega_{gs}(\mu,\mu_{gs})}\,.

The resummation kernels are defined in Eq. (
203
). For compactness, in the subscripts of scales
\mu_{X}
and kernels
K_{X}
and
\omega_{X}
, we use the shorthand notation
gs
,
cs_{g}
and
c
for the symbols
S_{G}^{q}
,
S_{c_{g}}^{q}
and
\mathcal{C}^{q}
respectively. The functions are RG evolved from their respective scales
\mu_{i}
to a common scale
\mu
, where natural scales for
e^{+}e^{-}
case are

\displaystyle\mu_{N}^{\rm can.}=Q\tan\frac{R}{2}\,,\>\>\,\;\;\mu_{gs}^{\rm can.}=Q_{\rm cut}\tan^{1+\beta}\frac{R}{2}\,,\>\>\,\;\;\mu_{cs_{g}}^{\rm can.}=Q_{\rm cut}\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\Big)^{1+\beta}\,,\>\>\,\;\;\mu_{c}^{\rm can.}=Q\frac{R_{g}}{2}\,.

(62)

Here the superscript ‘can.’ denotes the canonical choice of the scale.

The function
\mathcal{C}^{\kappa}
that appears in Eq. (
60
) is a generalization of Eq. (
3.3
) which includes two-loop terms, given by

\displaystyle\frac{m_{J}^{2}}{\big(Q\frac{R_{g}}{2}\big)^{2}}\,{\cal C}^{q}\bigg[\frac{4m_{J}^{2}}{Q^{2}R_{g}^{2}},\frac{QR_{g}}{2},\mu\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{\pi}a_{10}^{\cal C}\Big(\frac{\theta_{c}^{2}}{R_{g}^{2}}\Big)\Bigg[1+\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{\pi}\bigg(\frac{1}{4}\textrm{ln}^{2}\frac{4\mu^{2}}{Q^{2}R_{g}^{2}}+\frac{3}{4}\textrm{ln}\frac{4\mu^{2}}{Q^{2}R_{g}^{2}}\bigg)

(63)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\alpha_{s}\beta_{0}}{4\pi}\textrm{ln}\frac{4\mu^{2}}{Q^{2}R_{g}^{2}}\Bigg]+\Big(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\pi}a_{20}^{\cal C}\Big)\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{F}}{\pi}a_{10}^{\cal C}\Big(\frac{64}{25}\frac{(\theta_{c}/2)^{2}}{R_{g}^{2}}\Big)\,+{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})\,.

Following the notation in Tab.
1
we have identified the LO small
R_{g}
distribution for non-zero jet masses as

\displaystyle a_{10}^{\cal C}\Big(\frac{\theta_{c}^{2}}{R_{g}^{2}}\Big)\equiv\Theta\Big(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\theta_{c}}{R_{g}}\Big)\Bigg[2\,\textrm{ln}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{1-\frac{4\theta_{c}^{2}}{R_{g}^{2}}}\bigg)-\frac{3}{4}\sqrt{1-\frac{4\theta_{c}^{2}}{R_{g}^{2}}}-\textrm{ln}\Big(\frac{\theta_{c}^{2}}{R_{g}^{2}}\Big)\Bigg]\,.

(64)

In the square brackets of Eq. (
63
) we have included the logarithms of
\mu/(QR_{g}/2)
that are generated at two-loops due to the anomalous dimension of the
{\cal C}^{q}
function and the running coupling. Furthermore, we have switched to the distribution differential in
\textrm{ln}\,m^{2}_{J}
, where the Jacobian factor
m_{J}^{2}
is absorbed (in the case of quarks) in the combination
turning the distributions in Eq. (
3.3
) into ordinary functions, and leading to the expression in Eq. (
64
) for the LO distribution.
The parameter
a_{20}^{\cal C}
is included in order to parameterize the uncertainty resulting from our lack of knowledge of the two-loop non-logarithmic corrections in the small
R_{g}
regime. For this uncertainty analysis we are making an approximation that the non-logarithmic corrections have the same functional form as the leading order distribution. At the same time, the modified argument of the function
a_{10}^{\cal C}
in the second line reflects the difference in the end points of the LO and NLO distributions. In the LO distribution in Eq. (
3.3
) the end point of the
R_{g}
distribution is given by
R_{g}^{\rm min}|_{\rm LO}=2\theta_{c}
. In contrast, the calculation of minimization of
R_{g}
for a given jet mass for three particle configuration at NLO yields an end point at
R_{g}^{\rm min}|_{\rm NLO}=(8/5)\theta_{c}
in the small angle limit, which was computed in appendix B of Ref.
Marzani:2017mva
. In Sec.
6
we will vary the parameter
a_{20}^{\cal C}
in the range
[-2\pi,2\pi]
to obtain an estimate of the uncertainty from missing
{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})
terms.

We now state the result for
pp
collisions:

\displaystyle\frac{d\Sigma(R_{g})}{d(\textrm{ln}\,m_{J}^{2})d\Phi_{J}}=\frac{m_{J}^{2}}{\big(p_{T}R_{g}\big)^{2}}\sum_{\kappa=q,g}N^{\kappa}_{\rm evol}(\Phi_{J},Q_{\rm cut},\beta,R,\mu;\mu_{N},\mu_{gs})\>e^{\left[K_{cs_{g}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{g}})+K_{c}(\mu,\mu_{c})\right]}

(65)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\frac{\mu_{cs_{g}}}{R_{g}^{1+\beta}p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime}}\Big)^{\omega_{cs_{g}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{g}})}\Big(\frac{\mu_{c}}{p_{T}R_{g}}\Big)^{\omega_{c}(\mu,\mu_{c})}S_{c_{g}}^{\kappa}\!\Big[R_{g}(p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\mu_{cs_{g}}\Big]{\cal C}^{\kappa}\bigg[\frac{m_{J}^{2}}{p_{T}^{2}R_{g}^{2}},p_{T}R_{g},\mu\bigg]\Xi_{C}^{\kappa}(t_{C})\,,

where we follow the same substitutions of the arguments as described below Eq. (
56
).
Additionally, the first argument
\theta_{c}/R_{g}
in
{\cal C}_{q}
in Eq. (
63
) can be simply replaced by
\theta_{c}^{(pp)}/R_{g}
with
\theta_{c}^{(pp)}
defined in Eq. (
14
).
The normalization factor is now given by

\displaystyle N_{\kappa}^{\rm evol}(\Phi_{J},Q_{\rm cut},

\displaystyle\beta,R,\mu;\mu_{N},\mu_{gs})\equiv N^{\kappa}(\Phi_{J},R,\mu_{N})\,S_{G}^{\kappa}\Big(p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime}R^{1+\beta},\beta,\mu_{gs}\Big)\,\Xi_{G}^{\kappa}(t_{G})

\displaystyle\qquad\times e^{K_{N^{\kappa}}(\mu,\mu_{N})+K_{gs}(\mu,\mu_{gs})}\Big(\frac{\mu_{N}}{p_{T}R}\Big)^{\omega_{N^{\kappa}}(\mu,\mu_{N})}\Big(\frac{\mu_{gs}}{R^{1+\beta}p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime}}\Big)^{\omega_{gs}(\mu,\mu_{gs})}\,.

(66)

Accordingly, the natural scales for the
pp
case are

\displaystyle\mu_{N}^{\rm can.}=p_{T}R\,,\>\>\,\;\;\mu_{gs}^{\rm can.}=p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime}R^{1+\beta}\,,\>\>\,\;\;\mu_{cs_{g}}^{\rm can.}=p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime}R_{g}^{1+\beta}\,,\>\>\,\;\;\mu_{c}^{\rm can.}=p_{T}R_{g}\,.

(67)

4.2
Intermediate
R_{g}

Resummation in this regime is conveniently performed in Laplace space, where convolutions over the groomed jet mass reduce to ordinary products. For the intermediate
R_{g}
regime we have

\displaystyle\Sigma(m_{J}^{2},R_{g})

\displaystyle=N_{q}(Q,R,\mu)\,S_{G}^{q}\Big(Q_{\rm cut}\tan^{1+\beta}\frac{R}{2},\beta,\mu,R\Big)\,\Xi_{G}^{q}(t_{G})\,S_{c_{g}}^{q}\!\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Xi_{S}^{q}(t_{S})\int_{0}^{m_{J}^{2}}ds\int_{0}^{s/Q}\frac{d\ell^{+}}{R_{g}/2}\>J_{q}\big(s-Q\ell^{+},\mu\big)S_{c_{m}}^{q}\Big(\frac{\ell^{+}}{R_{g}/2},\mu\Big)\,

\displaystyle=N_{q}(Q,R,\mu)\,S_{G}^{q}\Big(Q_{\rm cut}\tan^{1+\beta}\frac{R}{2},\beta,\mu,R\Big)\,\Xi_{G}^{q}(t_{G})\,S_{c_{g}}^{q}\!\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Xi_{S}^{q}(t_{S})\int_{0}^{m_{J}^{2}}ds\int\frac{dx}{2\pi i}\,e^{xs}\>\tilde{J}_{q}(x,\mu)\,\tilde{S}_{c_{m}}^{q}\Big(\frac{xQR_{g}}{2},\mu\Big)\,,

(68)

where
\tilde{J}_{q}
and
\tilde{S}^{q}_{c_{m}}
are the Laplace transforms of
J_{q}
and
S^{q}_{c_{m}}
respectively. The one-loop results for
\tilde{J}_{\kappa}
and
\tilde{S}^{\kappa}_{c_{m}}
are provided in Eqs. (
224
) and (
234
) respectively.
Details of the Laplace transform between momentum and position space are presented in App.
A.1
.

At NLL
′
accuracy, the simple one-loop expressions of the functions in Eq. (
4.2
) make it straightforward to take the inverse Laplace transform.
To simplify the final formulae after taking inverse Laplace transform, we introduce an alternative notation for the Laplace space expressions,

\displaystyle\tilde{J}_{\kappa}\big[\log(e^{\gamma_{E}}x\mu_{J}^{2}),\alpha_{s}(\mu_{J})\big]

\displaystyle\equiv\tilde{J}_{\kappa}(x,\mu_{J})\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{c_{m}}^{\kappa}\big[\log(e^{\gamma_{E}}u\mu_{cs_{m}}),\alpha_{s}(\mu_{cs_{m}})\big]

\displaystyle\equiv\tilde{S}_{c_{m}}^{\kappa}(u,\mu_{cs_{m}})\,,

(69)

where we notice that the dependence of the functions on the r.h.s. on their first argument is purely logarithmic.
In Eq. (
4.2
) we have made explicit the Laplace space logarithms and the factors of running coupling.
In terms of this notation, the cross section (for
m_{J}^{2}>0
) with RG evolution made explicit reads

\displaystyle\frac{d\Sigma(R_{g})}{dm_{J}^{2}}

\displaystyle=N_{q}^{\rm evol}(Q,Q_{\rm cut},\beta,R,\mu;\mu_{N},\mu_{gs})\Bigg(\Xi_{S}^{q}(t_{S})\frac{d\Sigma_{\rm int}^{q}(R_{g},\partial_{\Omega})}{dm_{J}^{2}}\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}}{\Gamma(-\Omega)}

(70)

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg(m_{J}^{2}\frac{d}{dm_{J}^{2}}\Xi_{S}^{q}(t_{S})\bigg)\frac{d\Sigma_{\rm int}^{q}(R_{g},\partial_{\Omega})}{dm_{J}^{2}}\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}}{\Gamma(1-\Omega)}\Bigg)\bigg|_{\Omega=\tilde{\omega}(\mu_{cs_{m}},\mu_{J})}\,,

where

\displaystyle\frac{d\Sigma_{\rm int}^{q}(R_{g},\partial_{\Omega})}{dm_{J}^{2}}\equiv\frac{1}{m_{J}^{2}}\>e^{\big[K_{cs_{g}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{g}})+K_{cs_{m}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{m}})+K_{J}(\mu,\mu_{J})\big]}

(71)

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\frac{\mu_{cs_{g}}}{Q_{\rm cut}(R_{g}/2)^{1+\beta}}\Big)^{\omega_{cs_{g}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{g}})}\Big(\frac{\mu_{J}^{2}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big)^{\omega_{J}(\mu,\mu_{J})}\Big(\frac{QR_{g}\mu_{cs_{m}}}{2m_{J}^{2}}\Big)^{\omega_{cs_{m}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{m}})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\,S_{c_{g}}^{q}\!\Big[\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\mu_{cs_{g}}\Big]\tilde{J}_{q}\Big[\partial_{\Omega}+\log\Big(\frac{\mu_{J}^{2}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big),\,\alpha_{s}(\mu_{J})\Big]\,\tilde{S}_{c_{m}}^{q}\Big[\partial_{\Omega}+\log\Big(\frac{QR_{g}\mu_{cs_{m}}}{2m_{J}^{2}}\Big),\alpha_{s}(\mu_{cs_{m}})\Big]\,,

with the same prefactor
N^{\kappa}_{\rm evol}
defined in Eq. (
61
).
We have identified the RG invariant single logarithmic kernel
\tilde{\omega}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}(\mu_{cs_{m}},\mu_{J})=\omega_{cs_{m}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{m}})+\omega_{J}(\mu,\mu_{J})\,.

(72)

The first argument involving the derivative
\partial_{\Omega}
in the Laplace transforms of the jet and the collinear-soft functions in Eq. (
71
) is understood to replace the logarithms that appear in the corresponding Laplace space expressions in the notation described in Eq. (
4.2
). In the subscript
\mu_{cs_{m}}
we introduced the shorthand label
cs_{m}
for the symbol
S_{c_{m}}^{q}
. The collinear scale in Eq. (
62
) is now replaced by the two mass-dependent canonical scales

\displaystyle\mu_{cs_{m}}^{\rm can.}=\frac{2m_{J}^{2}}{QR_{g}}\,,\qquad\mu_{J}^{\rm can.}=m_{J}\,.

(73)

In the small
R_{g}
limit when
R_{g}=\theta_{c}=2m_{J}/Q
both of these scales merge such that
\mu_{cs_{m}}^{\rm can}=\mu_{J}^{\rm can.}=\mu_{c}^{\rm can.}=m_{J}
.
Indeed, as required by RG consistency, the evolution factors in Eq. (
71
) reduce to the ones in Eq. (
60
) in this limit.

Furthermore, we can simplify the derivative of
\Xi_{S}(t_{S})
in Eq. (
70
) using Eqs. (
29
) and (
44
) to arrive at the following simple expression:

\displaystyle m_{J}^{2}\frac{d}{m_{J}^{2}}\Xi_{S}\big(t[\mu_{cs_{g}},\mu_{cs_{m}}]\big)

\displaystyle=\Big(m_{J}^{2}\frac{d}{m_{J}^{2}}\mu_{cs_{m}}\Big)\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu_{cs_{m}}}t[\mu_{cs_{g}},\mu_{cs_{m}}]\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\Xi_{S}(t)\bigg|_{t=t_{S}}

(74)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu_{cs_{m}})}{2\pi}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\Xi_{S}(t)\bigg|_{t=t_{S}}\,,

where in the second line we made use of the canonical scale choice for
\mu_{cs_{m}}
in Eq. (
73
). One obtains a yet simpler expression when approximating
\Xi_{S}(t)
by the two-loop result in Eq. (
285
).

Lastly, the generalization for
pp
collisions is straightforward, with the factorization formula given by

\displaystyle\frac{d\Sigma(R_{g})}{dm_{J}^{2}d\Phi_{J}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\kappa=q,g}N^{\kappa}_{\rm evol}(\Phi_{J},Q_{\rm cut},\beta,R,\mu;\mu_{N},\mu_{gs})\Bigg(\Xi_{S}^{\kappa}(t_{S})\frac{d\Sigma_{\rm int}^{\kappa(pp)}(R_{g},\partial_{\Omega})}{dm_{J}^{2}d\Phi_{J}}\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}}{\Gamma(-\Omega)}

(75)

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg(m_{J}^{2}\frac{d}{dm_{J}^{2}}\Xi_{S}^{\kappa}(t_{S})\bigg)\frac{d\Sigma_{\rm int}^{\kappa(pp)}(R_{g},\partial_{\Omega})}{dm_{J}^{2}d\Phi_{J}}\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}}{\Gamma(1-\Omega)}\Bigg)\bigg|_{\Omega=\tilde{\omega}(\mu_{cs_{m}},\mu_{J})}\,,

with

\displaystyle\frac{d\Sigma_{\rm int}^{\kappa(pp)}(R_{g},\partial_{\Omega})}{dm_{J}^{2}d\Phi_{J}}=\frac{1}{m_{J}^{2}}\>e^{\big[K_{cs_{g}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{g}})+K_{cs_{m}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{m}})+K_{J}(\mu,\mu_{J})\big]}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big(\frac{\mu_{cs_{g}}}{p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime}R_{g}^{1+\beta}}\Big)^{\omega_{cs_{g}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{g}})}\Big(\frac{\mu_{J}^{2}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big)^{\omega_{J}(\mu,\mu_{J})}\Big(\frac{p_{T}R_{g}\mu_{cs_{m}}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big)^{\omega_{cs_{m}}(\mu,\mu_{cs_{m}})}S_{c_{g}}^{\kappa}\!\Big[R_{g}(p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\mu_{cs_{g}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\times\,\tilde{J}_{\kappa}\Big[\partial_{\Omega}+\log\Big(\frac{\mu_{J}^{2}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big),\,\alpha_{s}(\mu_{J})\Big]\,\tilde{S}_{c_{m}}^{\kappa}\Big[\partial_{\Omega}+\log\Big(\frac{p_{T}R_{g}\mu_{cs_{m}}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big),\alpha_{s}(\mu_{cs_{m}})\Big]\,,

(76)

where we use the normalization factor for the
pp
case in Eq. (
4.1
).
The generalization for canonical scale
\mu_{cs_{m}}
for
pp
case is given by

\displaystyle\mu_{cs_{m}}^{\rm can.}=\frac{m_{J}^{2}}{p_{T}R_{g}}\,\qquad(\text{$pp$ case})\,,

(77)

and the same formula for the jet scale in Eq. (
73
) applies here. We note that given our convention for Laplace transforms in Eqs. (
223
) and (
233
), precisely the same functions
\tilde{J}_{\kappa}
and
\tilde{S}_{c_{m}}^{\kappa}
as in Eqs. (
70
) and (
71
) appear here (with a possibility for
\kappa=g
).

4.3
Large
R_{g}

We now derive a formula for the resummed cross section in the region
\theta_{c}^{2}\ll R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}(m_{J}^{2})
. The factorization structure and the one-loop ingredients relevant to this regime were discussed in Sec.
3.1
. NLL
′
resummation requires introducing a number of new functions, whose notation we describe below, while leaving presentation of the full expressions to App.
B.1
.

The key ingredient of our analysis is the collinear-soft function displayed at one loop in Eq. (
34
). Since its anomalous dimension is unaffected by the
R_{g}
measurement, it has the same RG evolution as the collinear-soft function that enters the single differential jet mass distribution. At the same time, the
R_{g}
dependence only arises at one loop, which makes the
\mathcal{O}(\alpha_{s})
correction to the collinear-soft function the leading order contribution to the double differential cross section. This motivates rewriting the collinear-soft function in the following multiplicative manner:

\displaystyle S_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr]

\displaystyle=\int d\ell^{\prime+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}S_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl[(\ell^{+}-\ell^{\prime+})Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr]

(78)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Delta S_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl[\ell^{\prime+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta\Bigr]\,,

where the collinear-soft function
S_{c}^{\kappa}[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu]
is singly differential in the jet mass and is responsible for RG evolution, whereas the
R_{g}
dependent corrections are incorporated via
\Delta S_{c}^{\kappa}
, which is by construction independent of the renormalization scale.
Eq. (
78
) is just a reorganization of the perturbative series. However, including one-loop corrections in both functions on the r.h.s. allows us to supplement our predictions with terms of the form
\{
leading RG logarithms
\}\times\{
leading
R_{g}
corrections
\}
, which when taken together first appear at
\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{2}_{s})
. In fact, we define NLL
′
accuracy for the double differential distribution in the large
R_{g}
region based on this reorganization. However, we stress that such a prescription does not capture all
\mathcal{O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})
terms in the collinear-soft function and in particular misses the genuine two-loop
R_{g}
dependent corrections.
We obtain an estimate for the uncertainty from these missing two-loop terms via the procedure described below.
To make the
\Delta S_{c}^{\kappa}
term in Eq. (
78
) explicitly independent of
\mu
, we can further rewrite it as

\displaystyle\Delta S_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta\Bigr]=\delta\Bigl(\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\Bigr)

(79)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int\frac{d\ell^{\prime+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\big)^{2+\beta}}\>\Delta S_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}\Bigl[(\ell^{+}-\ell^{\prime+})Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr]\>\Delta S_{c}^{[C_{\kappa}]}\Bigg[\frac{\ell^{\prime+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\big)^{2+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigg]\,,

where we have defined the one-loop contributions

\displaystyle\Delta S_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr]=\delta\Bigl(\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu)\beta_{0}}{2\pi}\Big(\frac{1+\beta}{2+\beta}\Big)\frac{1}{\mu^{\frac{2+\beta}{1+\beta}}}{\cal L}_{0}\!\biggl[\frac{\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}{\mu^{\frac{2+\beta}{1+\beta}}}\biggr]+{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})\,,

(80)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{c}^{[C_{\kappa}]}\Bigg[\tilde{\ell}=\frac{\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\big)^{2+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigg]=

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu)C_{\kappa}}{\pi}\frac{2}{2+\beta}\Theta(\tilde{\ell}-1)\frac{\log(\tilde{\ell})}{\tilde{\ell}}\bigg(1+\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu)}{\pi}a_{20}^{S_{c}}\bigg)+{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})\,.

(81)

The function
\Delta S_{c}^{[C_{\kappa}]}
carries the
R_{g}
dependent one-loop term in Eq. (
34
), while
\Delta S_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}
renders the convolution in Eq. (
79
)
\mu
independent at NLL
′
accuracy by compensating for the NLL running of the coupling in
\Delta S_{c}^{[C_{\kappa}]}
. This separation is again just a reshuffling of the perturbative series, as the
\mathcal{O}{(\alpha_{s}^{2})}
cross terms are predicted from RG consistency of the double differential cross section. The construction to make the
R_{g}
dependent correction
\Delta S_{c}^{\kappa}
in Eq. (
79
) explicitly independent of
\mu
can similarly be generalized at higher orders.
Finally, we note that expanding Eq. (
78
) to
{\cal O}(\alpha_{s})
we recover the one-loop expression of the collinear-soft function given above in Eq. (
34
). As a further check, the
R_{g}
dependent term vanishes when
R_{g}=\theta_{g}^{\star}
(
\tilde{\ell}=1
in Eq. (
81
)), and the collinear-soft function cumulative in
R_{g}
of Eq. (
78
) reduces in this limit to the collinear-soft function single differential in the groomed jet mass.

Since our treatment lacks the 2-loop non-logarithmic
R_{g}
dependent corrections, we have parametrized the resulting uncertainty in Eq. (
81
) via the parameter
a_{20}^{S_{c}}
which we will vary in our uncertainty analysis. This estimate utilizes an approximation that the two-loop corrections have the same functional form as that of the one-loop term, but with an unknown normalization. In the numerical studies presented below we will consider variation of
a_{20}^{S_{c}}
in the range
[-2\pi,2\pi]
. We will return to discussing this when we study the numerical results in Sec.
6
.

To carry out resummation in Laplace space the transform of the collinear-soft function is defined with respect to the variable
\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl(s,\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr)

\displaystyle=Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\int d\ell^{+}e^{-s\big(\ell^{+}\,Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\big)}S_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl[\ell^{+}\,Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr]\,.

(82)

Equations (
78
) and (
79
) in Laplace space read

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl(s,\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\bigl(s,\beta,\mu\bigr)\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl(s,\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta\Bigr)\,,

(83)

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl(s,\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta\Bigr)

\displaystyle=1+\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}\bigl(s,\beta,\mu\bigr)\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[C_{\kappa}]}\Bigl(s\,Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{2+\beta}{1+\beta}}\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\Big)^{2+\beta},\beta,\mu\Bigr)\,,

(84)

where the Laplace transforms of the individual terms are given in Eqs. (
238
) and (
239
). Hence, the cross section for
e^{+}e^{-}
collisions in the Laplace space takes on the form

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!dm_{J}^{2}\,\,e^{-x\,m_{J}^{2}}

\displaystyle\frac{d\Sigma(R_{g})}{dm_{J}^{2}}\bigg|_{R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}}=N_{q}(Q,R,\mu)\,S_{G}^{q}\Big(Q_{\rm cut}\tan^{1+\beta}\frac{R}{2},\beta,\mu,R\Big)\,\Xi_{G}^{q}(t_{G})\,

\displaystyle\times\tilde{J}_{q}(x,\mu)\,\tilde{S}_{c}^{q}\Bigl(xQQ_{\rm cut}^{-\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr)\>\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{q}\Bigl(xQQ_{\rm cut}^{-\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta\Bigr)\,.

(85)

In App.
B.2
we show that carrying out RG evolution in Laplace space and transforming back to momentum space yields the following result for the resummed cross section at NLL
′
accuracy for the product of terms on the second line of Eq. (
4.3
),

\displaystyle\frac{d\Sigma(R_{g})}{dm_{J}^{2}}\bigg|_{R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}}

\displaystyle=N^{q}_{\rm evol}\big(Q,Q_{\rm cut},\beta,R,\mu;\mu_{N},\mu_{gs}\big)\frac{d\sigma^{q}\big[\partial_{\Omega}\big]}{dm_{J}^{2}}\Bigg(\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}}{\Gamma(-\Omega)}+{\cal Q}^{q}\Big[\Omega,\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}},\beta\Big]\Bigg)\Bigg|_{\Omega=\tilde{\omega}(\mu_{cs},\mu_{J})}\,.

(86)

The function of the differential operator
\partial_{\Omega}
in Eq. (
86
) (along with the first term in brackets in Eq. (
86
)) coincides with the single differential jet mass cross section in its fully factorized form:

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{q}\big[\partial_{\Omega}\big]}{dm_{J}^{2}}\>

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{m_{J}^{2}}e^{\big[K_{cs}(\mu,\mu_{cs})+K_{J}(\mu,\mu_{J})\big]}\Big(\frac{\mu_{J}^{2}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big)^{\omega_{J}(\mu,\mu_{J})}\bigg(\frac{Q\mu_{cs}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big(\frac{\mu_{cs}}{Q_{\rm cut}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\bigg)^{\omega_{cs}(\mu,\mu_{cs})}

\displaystyle\times\tilde{J}_{q}\Big[\partial_{\Omega}+\log\Big(\frac{\mu_{J}^{2}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big),\,\alpha_{s}(\mu_{J})\Big]\,\tilde{S}_{c}^{q}\bigg[\partial_{\Omega}+\log\bigg(\frac{Q\mu_{cs}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big(\frac{\mu_{cs}}{Q_{\rm cut}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\bigg),\beta,\alpha_{s}(\mu_{cs})\bigg]\,,

(87)

where the Laplace space logarithms in the jet and collinear-soft functions are replaced by the argument in brackets.
Here, in analogy with Eq. (
4.2
), we have defined the alternative notation for the Laplace transform of the collinear-soft function

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\Big[\log\big(se^{\gamma_{E}}\mu^{\frac{2+\beta}{1+\beta}}\big),\beta,\alpha(\mu_{cs})\Big]\equiv\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\bigl(s,\beta,\mu_{cs}\bigr)\,,

(88)

with the explicit one-loop expression given in Eq. (
241
),
while in analogy with Eq. (
72
), we have introduced in Eq. (
86
) the RG invariant combination

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}(\mu_{cs},\mu_{J})\equiv\omega_{J}(\mu,\mu_{J})+\omega_{cs}(\mu,\mu_{cs})=\omega_{J}(\mu_{cs},\mu_{J})\,,

(89)

with the definition of
\omega_{J,S_{c}}
provided in Eq. (
203
).
The RG evolved normalization factor
N^{\kappa}_{\rm evol}
in Eq. (
4.3
) is the same as Eq. (
61
). Along with the scales that are in common with the small and intermediate
R_{g}
regimes in Eqs. (
62
) and (
73
), the logarithms in the
S_{c}^{\kappa}
and the corresponding RG evolution factors are minimized by the following choice of the collinear-soft scale:

\displaystyle\mu_{cs}^{\rm can.}=\Big(\frac{m_{J}^{2}}{Q}\Big)^{\frac{1+\beta}{2+\beta}}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{2+\beta}}\,.

(90)

Note that since all the scales and anomalous dimensions for this regime are independent of
R_{g}
, so is the function of the derivative operator in Eq. (
4.3
).

The effect of the cumulative
R_{g}
measurement on Eq. (
86
) is accounted for entirely by the kernel
{\cal Q}^{\kappa}
defined as

\displaystyle{\cal Q}^{\kappa}\Big[\Omega,\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}}\Big)^{2+\beta},\beta\Big]

\displaystyle\equiv e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}\bigg[\partial_{\Omega}+\log\bigg(\frac{Q\mu_{cs}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big(\frac{\mu_{cs}}{Q_{\rm cut}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\bigg),\beta,\alpha_{s}(\mu_{cs})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\int\frac{d\varrho}{2\pi i}\>e^{\varrho}\varrho^{\Omega}\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[C_{\kappa}]}\Bigg(\varrho\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}}\Big)^{2+\beta},\beta,\mu_{cs}\Bigg)\,,

(91)

which is
\mu
-independent by construction and vanishes at
R_{g}=\theta_{g}^{\star}(m_{J}^{2},Q_{\rm cut},\beta)
.
Here
\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}
is expressed in the notation equivalent to that in Eq. (
88
),

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}\Big[\log\big(se^{\gamma_{E}}\mu^{\frac{2+\beta}{1+\beta}}\big),\beta,\alpha(\mu_{cs})\Big]\equiv\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}\bigl(s,\beta,\mu_{cs}\bigr)\,,

(92)

see Eq. (
242
).
The explicit expression for
{\cal Q}^{\kappa}
is given in in Eq. (
249
). To make a connection with the notation introduced in Tab.
1
, we can identify the LO distribution in the large
R_{g}
regime that multiplies the NLL evolution factors in Eq. (
4.3
) as

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{\kappa}}{\pi}a_{10}^{S_{c}}\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}},\beta\Big)={\cal Q}^{\kappa}\Big[\tilde{\omega}(\mu_{cs},\mu_{J}),\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}(m_{J}^{2})},\beta\Big]+{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})\,.

(93)

Next, the result for
pp
case is given by

\displaystyle\frac{d\Sigma(R_{g})}{dm_{J}^{2}d\Phi_{J}}\bigg|_{R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\kappa=q,g}N^{\kappa}_{\rm evol}\big(\Phi_{J},Q_{\rm cut},\beta,R,\mu;\mu_{N},\mu_{gs}\big)

(94)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{d\sigma^{\kappa}\big[\partial_{\Omega}\big]}{dm_{J}^{2}d\Phi_{J}}\Bigg(\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}}{\Gamma(-\Omega)}+{\cal Q}^{\kappa}\Big[\Omega,\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{(pp)\star}(m_{J}^{2})},\beta\Big]\Bigg)\Bigg|_{\Omega=\tilde{\omega}(\mu_{cs},\mu_{J})}\,,

where

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma^{\kappa}\big[\partial_{\Omega}\big]}{dm_{J}^{2}d\Phi_{J}}\>

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{m_{J}^{2}}e^{\big[K_{cs}(\mu,\mu_{cs})+K_{J}(\mu,\mu_{J})\big]}\Big(\frac{\mu_{J}^{2}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big)^{\omega_{J}(\mu,\mu_{J})}\bigg(\frac{p_{T}\mu_{cs}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big(\frac{\mu_{cs}}{p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\bigg)^{\omega_{cs}(\mu,\mu_{cs})}

(95)

\displaystyle\times\tilde{J}_{\kappa}\Big[\partial_{\Omega}+\log\Big(\frac{\mu_{J}^{2}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big),\,\alpha_{s}(\mu_{J})\Big]\,\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\bigg[\partial_{\Omega}+\log\bigg(\frac{p_{T}\mu_{cs}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big(\frac{\mu_{cs}}{p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\bigg),\beta,\alpha_{s}(\mu_{cs})\bigg]\,.

Additionally, the same formula for
{\cal Q}^{\kappa}
in Eq. (
4.3
) applies but the arguments of
\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}
being the same as that of
\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}
in the last line of the equation above. In deriving this result we have used the following convention for Laplace transform of the collinear-soft function for
pp
case

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl(s,R_{g}(p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr)

(96)

\displaystyle\qquad=(p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\int dr^{+}e^{-s\big(r^{+}\,(p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\big)}S_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl[r^{+}(p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},R_{g}(p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr]\,,

and the analogs of Eqs. (
83
) and (
84
) can be derived by following the substitutions in Eq. (
38
).
Finally, we see that the natural scale choice for
\mu_{cs}
in
pp
collisions is given by

\displaystyle\mu_{cs}^{\rm can.}=\Big(\frac{m_{J}^{2}}{p_{T}}\Big)^{\frac{1+\beta}{2+\beta}}(p_{T}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2+\beta}}\,\qquad(\text{$pp$ case})\,.

(97)

4.4
Boundary corrections in the Large
R_{g}
region

Next we discuss boundary corrections to the soft drop cross section in the regime where
R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}(m_{J}^{2},Q_{\rm cut},\beta)
,
relevant for computing the
\varepsilon
derivative in Eq. (
5
).
We first compute the bare collinear-soft and global-soft functions with the shifted soft drop condition in Eq. (
6
). This will allow us to identify the relevant corrections to the double differential cross section at first order in the shift parameter
\varepsilon
. We will find that both the function of the derivative operator
d\sigma(\partial_{\Omega})
and the evolution kernel
{\cal Q}^{\kappa}
in Eqs. (
86
) and (
94
) receive
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
modifications.

4.4.1
Boundary corrections to bare soft matrix elements

The
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
corrections to the soft matrix elements lead to logarithmic and non-logarithmic terms proportional to
R_{g}^{-\beta}Q\varepsilon/Q_{\rm cut}
.
3
3

3

Note that we use
\varepsilon
for the shift to soft drop condition and
\epsilon
for the dimensional regularization parameter.

This can be seen from the bare result for the collinear-soft function with the shifted soft drop condition defined in Eq. (
6
) given by

\displaystyle Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}S_{c}^{\kappa[1],\rm bare}\bigl[\ell^{+},R_{g},Q_{\rm cut},\overline{\Theta}_{\rm sd}(\varepsilon),\beta,\mu\bigr]

(98)

\displaystyle\qquad\equiv\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{\kappa}}{\pi}\frac{(\mu^{2}e^{\gamma_{E}})^{\epsilon}}{\Gamma(1-\epsilon)}\int\frac{dp^{+}dp^{-}}{(p^{+}p^{-})^{1+\epsilon}}\overline{\Theta}_{\rm sd}(\varepsilon)\big[\overline{\Theta}_{R_{g}}\delta\big(\ell^{+}-p^{+}\big)-\delta(\ell^{+})\big]\,,

where the condition to pass or fail the
R_{g}
constraint are written respectively as

\displaystyle\overline{\Theta}_{R_{g}}\equiv\Theta\bigg(\frac{R_{g}}{2}-\Big(\frac{p^{+}}{p^{-}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)\,,\qquad\Theta_{R_{g}}\equiv 1-\overline{\Theta}_{R_{g}}\,.

(99)

Expanding

\displaystyle\overline{\Theta}_{\rm sd}(\varepsilon)=\overline{\Theta}_{\rm sd}+\varepsilon\delta_{\rm sd}+{\cal O}(\varepsilon^{2}),

(100)

where
\delta_{\rm sd}=\delta(z-\tilde{z}_{\rm cut}\theta^{\beta})
,
yields
4
4

4

We include the argument
\overline{\Theta}_{\rm sd}(\varepsilon)
to indicate that the corresponding functions are evaluated with the shifted soft drop condition in Eq. (
6
). We then indicate
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
boundary corrections to various functions by including a subscript
\varepsilon
to distinguish them from the
{\cal O}(\varepsilon^{0})
part.

\displaystyle S_{c}^{\kappa[1],\rm bare}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\overline{\Theta}_{\rm sd}(\varepsilon),\beta,\mu\Bigr]

\displaystyle=S_{c}^{\kappa[1],\rm bare}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr]

(101)

\displaystyle+\frac{Q\varepsilon}{Q_{\rm cut}}\,S_{c,\varepsilon}^{\kappa[1],\rm bare}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr]+{\cal O}(\varepsilon^{2})\,.

The
{\cal O}(\varepsilon^{0})
term in Eq. (
100
) simply results in the usual soft drop condition, returning the bare one-loop version of the collinear-soft function in Eq. (
34
), while the
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
correction is

\displaystyle Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}S_{c,\varepsilon}^{\kappa[1],\rm bare}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr]

(102)

\displaystyle\qquad\equiv\frac{2}{2+\beta}\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{\kappa}}{\pi}\Big(\frac{\mu}{Q_{\rm cut}}\Big)^{2\epsilon}\frac{(e^{\gamma_{E}})^{\epsilon}}{\Gamma(1-\epsilon)}\int\frac{dp^{+}}{Q_{\rm cut}}\Big(\frac{p^{+}}{Q_{\rm cut}}\Big)^{-\frac{2(1+\beta)}{2+\beta}(1+\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Bigg[\Theta\bigg(\frac{R_{g}}{2}-\Big(\frac{p^{+}}{Q_{\rm cut}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2+\beta}}\bigg)\big[\delta\big(\ell^{+}-p^{+}\big)-\delta(\ell^{+})\big]-\delta(\ell^{+})\Theta\bigg(\Big(\frac{p^{+}}{Q_{\rm cut}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2+\beta}}-\frac{R_{g}}{2}\bigg)\Bigg]\,.

We have used the
\delta_{\rm sd}
to perform the integral over
p^{-}
, and have isolated the contribution from the soft-drop failing piece, proportional to
\delta(\ell^{+})
, so as to regulate the integral for
p^{+}\rightarrow 0
. The term proportional to
\delta(\ell^{+})
in the third line is no longer scaleless as it is bounded from below. Solving the remaining integration, we find for
\beta=0
and
\beta>0
respectively:

\displaystyle S_{c,\varepsilon}^{\kappa[1],\rm bare}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta=0,\mu\Bigr]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{\kappa}}{\pi}\Bigg(-\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\rm UV}}\delta\big(\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}\big)+\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}{\cal L}_{0}\Big(\frac{\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}}{\mu^{2}}\Big)+\Theta\bigg(\frac{\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}\big)^{2}}-1\bigg)\frac{1}{\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}}\Bigg)\,,

\displaystyle S_{c,\varepsilon}^{\kappa[1],\rm bare}\Bigg[\tilde{\ell}=\frac{\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\big)^{2+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta>0,\mu\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2}{2+\beta}\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu)C_{\kappa}}{\pi}\frac{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\big)^{-(2+\beta)}}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\big)^{\beta}}\Bigg({\cal L}_{0}^{-\frac{\beta}{2+\beta}}(\tilde{\ell})+\frac{\Theta(\tilde{\ell}-1)}{\tilde{\ell}^{\frac{2(1+\beta)}{2+\beta}}}\Bigg)\,.

(103)

We see that for
\beta=0
the
\epsilon>0
regulator results in a UV pole, while for
\beta>0
the result is a power correction and dimensional regularization is not needed.
Here, we have defined
5
5

5

We note that for
a>0
the function
{\cal L}_{0}^{a}(x)
is equivalent to
1/x^{1-a}
which has an integrable singularity as
x\rightarrow 0
.

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{0}^{a}(x)\equiv{\cal L}^{a}(x)+\frac{1}{a}\delta(x)\,,\qquad{\cal L}^{a}(x)=\Big[\frac{\Theta(x)}{x^{1-a}}\Big]_{+}\,,\qquad a\neq 0\,.

(104)

We can carry out a similar calculation for the
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
boundary corrections to the global-soft function. In full analogy with Eq. (
101
), we find that up to
{\cal O}(\varepsilon^{2})
terms

\displaystyle S_{G}^{\kappa[1],\rm bare}\bigl(Q_{\rm cut},R,\beta,\Theta_{\rm sd}(\varepsilon),\mu\bigr)=S_{G}^{\kappa[1],\rm bare}\bigl(Q_{\rm cut},R,\beta,\mu\bigr)+\frac{Q\varepsilon}{Q_{\rm cut}}S_{G,\varepsilon}^{\kappa[1],\rm bare}\bigl(Q_{\rm cut},R,\beta,\mu\bigr)\,,

(105)

where the additional argument
\Theta_{\rm sd}(\varepsilon)
denotes now the shift in the soft drop
failing
condition. The result for the
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
correction to the bare global-soft function with shifted soft drop reads

\displaystyle S_{G,\varepsilon}^{\kappa[1],\rm bare}\bigl(Q_{\rm cut},R,\beta,\mu\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{\kappa}}{\pi}\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\bigg[\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\rm UV}}+2\log\Big(\frac{\mu}{Q_{\rm cut}\tan\frac{R}{2}}\Big)\bigg]&\quad\big(\beta=0\big)\\[10.0pt]
\bigg[\frac{2}{\beta}\frac{1}{\sin^{\beta}\big(\frac{R}{2}\big)}+\ldots\bigg]&\quad\big(\beta>0\big)\end{array}\right.\,,

where the additional terms not shown for
\beta>0
are
{\cal O}(1)
and not relevant to the discussion here.
The details of this derivation and complete results for
\beta>0
are provided in App.
B.3
.
We see that the additional single logarithmic UV divergence for
\beta=0
case has the opposite sign to that in Eq. (
4.4.1
), as required by RG consistency. Interestingly, we now find that for
\beta=0
the one-loop collinear-soft and global-soft functions develop a non-zero non-cusp anomalous dimension proportional to
\varepsilon
. With conventions for prefactors in anomalous dimensions, summarized in Eqs. (
206
) and (
209
), we find

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{S_{G}^{\kappa}}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut})=-\gamma_{0}^{S_{c}^{\kappa}}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut})\,\equiv\,\gamma_{0}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut})\,=\,8C_{\kappa}\frac{Q\varepsilon}{Q_{\rm cut}}\,\qquad(\beta=0)\,.

(108)

Hence, for
\beta=0
, the NLL
′
resummed cross section for shifted soft drop will involve additional running between the global-soft and collinear-soft virtualities due to this non-cusp anomalous dimension.
We now turn to addressing how the various
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
corrections are combined with the other EFT matrix elements so as to arrive at the result for resummed cross section with shifted soft drop condition.

4.4.2
Assembling the ingredients

Having found that the shift in the soft drop condition induces different
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
modifications to the collinear-soft function on the l.h.s. of Eq. (
78
),
we now generalize the decomposition in the r.h.s. of that equation to account for these additional boundary terms.
Since the correction for
\beta=0
consists of logarithms induced by the extra anomalous dimension in Eq. (
108
), we find it appropriate to combine this term with
S_{c}^{\kappa}\big[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\big]
in the r.h.s. of Eq. (
78
), which is responsible for RG evolution. On the other hand, the non-logarithmic,
R_{g}
dependent terms in Eq. (
4.4.1
) can be written as a function of the variable
\tilde{\ell}
and are treated in the same way as the
\Delta S_{c}^{[C_{\kappa}]}
piece in Eq. (
81
).
Thus, we express the renormalized collinear-soft function with cumulant
R_{g}
measurement and soft drop shift as follows:

\displaystyle S_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\overline{\Theta}_{\rm sd}(\varepsilon),\beta,\mu\Bigr]=\int d\ell^{\prime+}\,Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\,S_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl[(\ell^{+}-\ell^{\prime+})Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu,\,\delta_{\beta,0}\gamma_{0}^{S_{c}^{\kappa}}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut})\Bigr]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\int d\ell^{\prime\prime+}\Delta S_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl[(\ell^{\prime+}-\ell^{\prime\prime+})Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta\Bigr]\>\Delta S_{c,\varepsilon}^{\kappa}\Bigl[\ell^{\prime\prime+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta\Bigr]\,.

(109)

Here the collinear soft function for single differential jet mass measurement includes the additional logarithm that is generated for
\beta=0
in Eq. (
4.4.1
), as signaled by the extra argument
\delta_{\beta,0}\gamma_{0}^{S_{c}^{\kappa}}(\varepsilon z_{\rm cut})
, and is modified as follows:

\displaystyle S_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu,\,\delta_{\beta,0}\gamma_{0}^{S_{c}^{\kappa}}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut})\Bigr]\equiv S_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr]+\delta_{\beta,0}\frac{Q\varepsilon}{Q_{\rm cut}}\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{\kappa}}{\pi}\frac{1}{\mu^{2}}{\cal L}_{0}\Big(\frac{\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}}{\mu^{2}}\Big)\,,

(110)

while the term
\Delta S_{c,\varepsilon}^{\kappa}
is defined analogously to
\Delta S_{c}^{\kappa}
given in Eq. (
79
):

\displaystyle\Delta S_{c,\varepsilon}^{\kappa}\Bigl[\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta\Bigr]

\displaystyle=\delta\Bigl(\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\Bigr)+\int\frac{d\ell^{\prime+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\big)^{2+\beta}}\>\Delta S_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}\Bigl[(\ell^{+}-\ell^{\prime+})Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{Q\varepsilon}{Q_{\rm cut}}\frac{1}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\big)^{\beta}}\Delta S_{c,\varepsilon}^{[C_{\kappa}]}\Bigg[\frac{\ell^{\prime+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\big)^{2+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigg]\,.

(111)

The
\Delta S_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}
piece is the same as that defined in Eq. (
80
) and is again included to cancel the
\mu
dependence due to the running coupling at NLL
′
.
Finally, from Eq. (
4.4.1
) we have

\displaystyle\Delta S_{c,\varepsilon}^{[C_{\kappa}]}\Bigg[\tilde{\ell}=\frac{\ell^{+}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\big)^{2+\beta}},\,\beta,\,\mu\Bigg]=

(112)

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{2}{2+\beta}\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu)C_{\kappa}}{\pi}\Bigg(\Theta(\beta>0)\>{\cal L}_{0}^{-\frac{\beta}{2+\beta}}(\tilde{\ell})+\frac{\Theta(\tilde{\ell}-1)}{\tilde{\ell}^{\frac{2(1+\beta)}{2+\beta}}}\Bigg)\bigg(1+\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu)}{\pi}a_{20,\varepsilon}^{S_{c}}\bigg)+{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{2})\,.

Here the
\Theta(\beta>0)
signifies that the first term is only present for the
\beta>0
case, whereas the second term applies for all
\beta\geq 0
. As we shall see later, this second term contributes to the bulk of the boundary corrections, and hence to
C_{2}(m_{J}^{2})
.

Note that in defining
\Delta S_{c,\varepsilon}^{\kappa}
in this way we factored out the
R_{g}^{-\beta}
power present in Eq. (
4.4.1
) for
\beta>0
. Comparing this scaling with the
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
correction to the global-soft function in Eq. (
4.4.1
), we see that the
R_{g}^{-\beta}
power dominates the
\sin^{-\beta}(R/2)
power in the
R_{g}\ll R
approximation which is formally still valid in the large
R_{g}
region. Thus we expect boundary contributions to the global-soft piece (for
\beta>0
) to be numerically insignificant, and we will leave them out from our numerical predictions.
Finally, since we lack the knowledge of the non-logarithmic two-loop boundary corrections in
S_{c}^{\kappa}
that cannot be predicted by the anomalous dimensions we have included an additional parameter
a_{20,\varepsilon}^{S_{c}}
in spirit of Eq. (
81
), to facilitate estimating the uncertainty resulting from these terms. We will come back to this again in Sec.
6
when studying the perturbative uncertainties.

Taking the Laplace transform of Eq. (
4.4.2
), performing an expansion up to O(
\varepsilon
), and dropping terms that are
{\cal O}(\alpha_{s}^{3})
we explicitly get

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl(s,\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\overline{\Theta}_{\rm sd}(\varepsilon),\beta,\mu\Bigr)=\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\Bigl(s,\frac{R_{g}}{2}Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}},\beta,\mu\Bigr)

(113)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{Q\varepsilon}{Q_{\rm cut}}\bigg\{-\delta_{\beta,0}\frac{\alpha_{s}C_{\kappa}}{\pi}\log(se^{\gamma_{E}}\mu^{2})\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[C_{\kappa}]}\Bigl(s\,Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{2+\beta}{1+\beta}}\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\Big)^{2+\beta},\beta,\mu\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\big)^{\beta}}\>\tilde{S}_{c}^{\kappa}\bigl(s,\beta,\mu\bigr)\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}\bigl(s,\beta,\mu\bigr)\Delta\tilde{S}_{c,\varepsilon}^{[C_{\kappa}]}\Bigl(s\,Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{2+\beta}{1+\beta}}\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\Big)^{2+\beta},\beta,\mu\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\big)^{\beta}}\>\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[C_{\kappa}]}\Bigl(s\,Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{2+\beta}{1+\beta}}\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\Big)^{2+\beta},\beta,\mu\Bigr)\Delta\tilde{S}^{[C_{\kappa}]}_{c,\varepsilon}\Bigl(s\,Q_{\rm cut}^{\frac{2+\beta}{1+\beta}}\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\Big)^{2+\beta},\beta,\mu\Bigr)\bigg\}\,,

where we used Eqs. (
83
) and (
84
) to expand the Laplace transform of the
\Delta S_{c}^{\kappa}
term, while the Laplace transform of the
\Delta S_{c,\varepsilon}^{[C_{\kappa}]}
term is given in Eq. (
260
). The first line reproduces the
{\cal O}(\varepsilon^{0})
result in Eq. (
78
) and vanishes when taking the
\varepsilon
derivative in Eq. (
5
). The second line describes boundary corrections induced by the additional logarithm present for
\beta=0
, while the remaining lines encode boundary corrections that are
R_{g}
dependent. The product of the two
\Delta S_{c}^{[C_{\kappa}]}
functions in the last line is important to ensure a smooth transition to the regime
R_{g}\ll\theta_{g}^{\star}
, which justifies including the boundary corrections
\Delta S_{c,\varepsilon}^{\kappa}
multiplicatively in Eq. (
4.4.2
).

Likewise, we express the global-soft function with
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
corrections as

\displaystyle S_{G}^{\kappa}\bigl(Q_{\rm cut},R,

\displaystyle\overline{\Theta}_{\rm sd}(\varepsilon),\beta,\mu\bigr)=S_{G}^{\kappa}\Big(Q_{\rm cut}\tan^{1+\beta}\frac{R}{2},\beta,\mu_{gs},R\Big)+\delta_{\beta,0}\frac{Q\varepsilon}{Q_{\rm cut}}\frac{2\alpha_{s}C_{\kappa}}{\pi}\log\Big(\frac{\mu}{Q_{\rm cut}\tan\frac{R}{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{Q\varepsilon}{Q_{\rm cut}}\frac{\Theta(\beta>0)}{\sin^{\beta}\big(\frac{R}{2}\big)}\>S_{G}^{\kappa}\Big(Q_{\rm cut}\tan^{1+\beta}\frac{R}{2},\beta,\mu_{gs},R\Big)\Delta S_{G,\varepsilon}^{\kappa}\bigl(Q_{\rm cut},R,\beta\bigr)\,,

(114)

with the result for
\mu
-independent
\Delta S_{G,\varepsilon}^{\kappa}
piece given in Eq. (
259
).

4.4.3
Cumulative boundary cross section

With all the ingredients for the shifted soft drop condition at hand, we are finally in the position to write resummed results for the cumulative cross section. Eq. (
4.3
) is still formally valid, but the global soft and collinear-soft functions that appear there now include boundary corrections according to Eqs. (
4.4.2
) and (
4.4.2
). As a consequence, the equivalent of Eq. (
86
) has two kind of terms:

\displaystyle\frac{d\Sigma(R_{g},\overline{\Theta}_{\rm sd}(\varepsilon))}{dm_{J}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{d\Sigma\big(R_{g},\delta_{\beta,0}\gamma_{0}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut})\big)}{dm_{J}^{2}}+\frac{Q\varepsilon}{Q_{\rm cut}}\frac{d\Delta\Sigma_{\varepsilon}(R_{g})}{dm_{J}^{2}}+{\cal O}(\varepsilon^{2})\,.

(115)

They describe boundary corrections to the NLL RG evolution and the
R_{g}
dependence respectively, and we now examine them in turn.
The first term reduces to the
{\cal O}(\varepsilon^{0})
cross section for positive
\beta
but, for
\beta=0
, includes the additional one-loop fixed order logarithms and running due to the
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
one-loop non-cusp anomalous dimension displayed in Eq. (
108
).
Since these corrections do not depend on the jet mass, they can be absorbed in a generalization of the operator
d\sigma^{q}(\partial_{\Omega})
in Eq. (
4.3
) yielding

\displaystyle\frac{d\Sigma\big(R_{g},\delta_{\beta,0}\gamma_{0}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut})\big)}{dm_{J}^{2}}\bigg|_{R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}}

\displaystyle\equiv N^{q}_{\rm evol}\big(Q,Q_{\rm cut},\beta,R,\mu;\mu_{N},\mu_{gs}\big)

(116)

\displaystyle\times\frac{d\sigma^{q}\big[\partial_{\Omega},\delta_{\beta,0}\gamma_{0}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut})\big]}{dm_{J}^{2}}\>\Bigg(\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}}{\Gamma(-\Omega)}+{\cal Q}^{q}\Big[\Omega,\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}},\beta\Big]\Bigg)\bigg|_{\Omega=\tilde{\omega}(\mu_{cs},\mu_{J})}\,,

while the evolution kernel in brackets is unchanged.
The operator is obtained from the following
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
modifications to its analogue in Eq. (
4.3
):

1.

The collinear-soft function in Eq. (
86
) includes the additional
\cal{O}(\varepsilon)
logarithm in Eq. (
113
).

2.

The global-soft function includes the additional
\cal{O}(\varepsilon)
logarithm in Eq. (
4.4.2
).

3.

The collinear-soft function has additional running:

\displaystyle K_{cs}\big(\mu,\mu_{cs},\delta_{\beta,0}\gamma^{S_{c}^{q}}_{0}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut})\big)

\displaystyle=K_{cs}(\mu,\mu_{cs})-\delta_{\beta,0}\,\eta\Big(\gamma_{0}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut}),\mu,\mu_{0}\Big)\,,

(117)

where the LL single logarithmic evolution kernel
\eta(\Gamma,\mu,\mu_{0})
is given in Eq. (
204
) from the one-loop non-cusp anomalous dimension.

4.

The normalization factor
N^{q}_{\rm evol}
has additional running due to the global soft function:

\displaystyle K_{gs}\big(\mu,\mu_{gs},\delta_{\beta,0}\gamma^{S_{G}^{q}}_{0}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut})\big)

\displaystyle=K_{gs}(\mu,\mu_{gs})+\delta_{\beta,0}\,\eta\big(\gamma_{0}(\varepsilon,z_{\rm cut}),\mu,\mu_{0}\big)\,.

(118)

The second term in Eq. (
115
) consists of the residual
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
non-logarithmic,
R_{g}
-dependent corrections to the collinear-soft and global-soft functions in Eqs. (
4.4.2
) and (
4.4.2
), which apply for every
\beta\geq 0
.
Since these terms do not alter evolution, but have a nontrivial dependence on the jet mass and
R_{g}
, they require a different kernel than Eq. (
4.3
):

\displaystyle{\cal Q}^{\kappa}_{\varepsilon}\bigg[\Omega,\upsilon=\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}}\Big)^{2+\beta},\beta\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[\beta_{0}]}\bigg[\partial_{\Omega}+\log\bigg(\frac{Q\mu_{cs}}{m_{J}^{2}}\Big(\frac{\mu_{cs}}{Q_{\rm cut}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{1+\beta}}\bigg),\beta,\alpha_{s}(\mu_{cs})\bigg]

(119)

\displaystyle\times\int\frac{d\varrho}{2\pi i}\>e^{\varrho}\varrho^{\Omega}\Delta\tilde{S}_{c,\varepsilon}^{[C_{\kappa}]}\big(\varrho\upsilon,\beta,\mu_{cs}\big)\Big(1+\Delta\tilde{S}_{c}^{[C_{\kappa}]}\big(\varrho\upsilon,\beta,\mu_{cs}\big)\Big)\,,

but use the same function of the derivative operator,
d\sigma^{\kappa}(\partial_{\Omega})
defined in Eq. (
4.3
), valid for
\varepsilon=0
:

\displaystyle\frac{d\Delta\Sigma_{\varepsilon}(R_{g})}{dm_{J}^{2}}\bigg|_{R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}}

\displaystyle=N^{q}_{\rm evol}\big(Q,Q_{\rm cut},\beta,R,\mu;\mu_{N},\mu_{gs}\big)\frac{d\sigma^{q}\big[\partial_{\Omega}\big]}{dm_{J}^{2}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\big)^{\beta}}\>{\cal Q}^{q}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\Omega,\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}},\beta\Big]

(120)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\Theta(\beta>0)}{\sin^{\beta}\big(\frac{R}{2}\big)}\>\Delta S_{G,\varepsilon}^{q}\bigl(Q_{\rm cut},R,\beta\bigr)\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}}{\Gamma(-\Omega)}\Bigg)\bigg|_{\Omega=\tilde{\omega}(\mu_{cs},\mu_{J})}\,.

The explicit expression of the evolution kernel
{\cal Q}^{\kappa}_{\varepsilon}
between jet and soft scales for shifted soft drop is given in Eq. (
B.3.2
).
Note that the term
\Delta S_{G,\varepsilon}^{\kappa}
for
\beta>0
at NLL
′
accuracy contributes only to the boundary correction in the single differential jet mass cross section, and is thus independent of
R_{g}
. In the following, we will simply ignore this term as this is also power suppressed compared to the term in the first line in Eq. (
120
).

We now briefly describe the generalization of the results above to
pp
collisions. First off, the proportionality constant of
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
terms is substituted as follows

\displaystyle\frac{Q}{Q_{\rm cut}\big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\big)^{\beta}}\rightarrow\frac{1}{R_{g}^{\beta}z_{\rm cut}^{\prime}}\,.

(121)

The same applies for the
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
one-loop non-cusp anomalous dimension in Eq. (
108
). Thus generalization of the cross section in Eq. (
116
) to the
pp
case is simply given by Eq. (
95
) after including the additional
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
logarithms and single log kernels as outlined above. The result for the second term in Eq. (
115
) for
pp
collisions can also be analogously derived from Eq. (
120
):

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{z_{\rm cut}^{\prime}}\frac{d\Delta\Sigma_{\varepsilon}(R_{g})}{dm_{J}^{2}d\Phi_{J}}\bigg|_{R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\kappa=q,g}N^{\kappa}_{\rm evol}\big(\Phi_{J},Q_{\rm cut},\beta,R,\mu;\mu_{N},\mu_{gs}\big)

(122)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{d\sigma^{\kappa}\big[\partial_{\Omega}\big]}{dm_{J}^{2}d\Phi_{J}}\Bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{z_{\rm cut}^{\prime}R_{g}^{\beta}}\>{\cal Q}^{\kappa}_{\varepsilon}\Big[\Omega,\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{(pp)\star}(m_{J}^{2})},\beta\Big]\Bigg)\bigg|_{\Omega=\tilde{\omega}(\mu_{cs},\mu_{J})}\,.

At this point, we have the soft drop boundary cross section which is cumulant in the groomed jet radius. In order to evaluate
C_{2}
, following Eq. (
5
), we need to switch to the cross section differential in the groomed jet radius and further take the
\varepsilon
derivative. We perform this explicitly in App.
B.4
, where we compile the results for the boundary cross section differential in
R_{g}
. The large
R_{g}
regime discussed so far is the only relevant geometry in the definition of
C^{q}_{2}(m_{J}^{2})
. For completeness, we discuss in App.
B.3
the boundary corrections in the intermediate and small
R_{g}
regimes and show consistency with the results in the large
R_{g}
regime as
R_{g}/\theta_{g}^{\star}(m_{J}^{2})\rightarrow 0
.

4.5
Recovering the Leading Logarithmic coherent branching result

In this section we make use of the results derived above for the cumulative cross section and the boundary corrections to demonstrate that
the EFT that we have developed reproduce the computation of
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
at LL accuracy within the coherent branching formulation from
Hoang:2019ceu
.
This also makes explicit how the EFT encodes the strong angular ordering limit used there. For simplicity we limit ourselves to the case of
e^{+}e^{-}
collisions here.
The LL results for
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
were derived by considering a chain of emissions that are ordered in their respective contribution to the jet mass.
6
6

6

Formally, the emissions are to be angular ordered, but in the derivation the angular ordering is replaced by their ordering in the jet mass, i.e. in the variable
\rho_{i}=z_{i}\theta_{i}^{2}
for the
i^{\rm th}
emission. This approximation is essential to set the resolution of the jet mass measurement.
The emission at the largest angle is required to satisfy soft drop and set the jet mass. This leads to the following expressions for the two moments:

\displaystyle C_{1}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{C_{0}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})}{m_{J}^{2}}\bigg)^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{R}\frac{d\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}}\>\frac{\theta}{2}\frac{C_{F}}{\pi}\alpha_{s}\Big(\frac{Qz\theta}{2}\Big)p_{gq}(z)e^{-{\cal R}(z\theta^{2})}

(123)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Theta\bigg(z-\frac{Q_{\rm cut}}{Q}\Big(\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)^{\beta}\bigg)\delta\Big(m_{J}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}z\theta^{2}Q^{2}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle C_{2}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{J}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\bigg(\frac{C_{0}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})}{m_{J}^{2}}\bigg)^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{R}\frac{d\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}}\>\frac{2}{\theta}\frac{C_{F}}{\pi}\alpha_{s}\Big(\frac{Qz\theta}{2}\Big)p_{gq}(z)e^{-{\cal R}(z\theta^{2})}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\delta\bigg(z-\frac{Q_{\rm cut}}{Q}\Big(\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)^{\beta}\bigg)\delta\Big(m_{J}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}z\theta^{2}Q^{2}\Big)\,,

where
C_{0}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})/m_{J}^{2}
is the single differential jet mass distribution and is given at this order by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{J}^{2}}C_{0}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})\equiv\frac{d\hat{\sigma}_{\rm LL}^{q}}{dm_{J}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{J}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{R}\frac{d\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}}\frac{C_{F}}{\pi}\alpha_{s}\Big(\frac{Qz\theta}{2}\Big)p_{gq}(z)e^{-{\cal R}(z\theta^{2})}

(124)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Theta\bigg(z-\frac{Q_{\rm cut}}{Q}\Big(\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)^{\beta}\bigg)\delta\Big(m_{J}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}z\theta^{2}Q^{2}\Big)\,,

where the result for
C_{0}(m_{J}^{2})
is valid up to LL.
In Eqs. (
123
) and (
124
) we have rewritten the soft drop condition in Eq. (
8
) terms of
Q_{\rm cut}
. In the expression for
C_{1}^{q}
and
C_{0}^{q}
, the condition is imposed on the last emission with energy fraction
z
and angle
\theta
.
In case of
C_{2}^{q}
we see, however, that the soft drop
\Theta
function is replaced by
\delta
function in order to capture the corrections at the soft drop boundary.
The radiator,
\mathcal{R}_{q}(\theta_{c}^{2})
, results from all the emissions at larger angles that are either vetoed by the jet mass measurement or that fail soft drop (and the virtual emissions). The radiator resums double logarithms of
\theta_{g}^{\star}/\theta_{c}
, which however cancel out in the ratio in Eqs. (
126
) and (
124
). The splitting kernel
p_{gq}
is given by

\displaystyle p_{gq}(z)=\frac{1+(1-z)^{2}}{2z}\underset{z\to 0}{\sim}\frac{1}{z}\,.

(125)

Terms that are power corrections in the
z\to 0
limit are formally beyond LL accuracy, and we will drop them in the following; in the EFT formulation, these are captured by the NLO corrections to the jet function. Simplifying Eq. (
123
) and using the LL limit of
p_{gq}(z)
in Eq. (
125
) we get

\displaystyle C_{0}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})\big|_{\rm LL}

\displaystyle=e^{-\mathcal{R}_{q}(\theta_{c}^{2})}\int_{\theta_{c}}^{R}\frac{d\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}}\frac{C_{F}}{\pi}\alpha_{s}\Big(\frac{m_{J}\theta_{c}}{\theta}\Big)\Theta(\theta_{g}^{\star}-\theta)\,,

(126)

\displaystyle C_{1}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})\big|_{\rm LL}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-\mathcal{R}(\theta_{c}^{2})}}{C_{0}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})}\int_{\theta_{c}}^{R}\frac{d\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}\frac{C_{F}}{\pi}\alpha_{s}\Big(\frac{m_{J}\theta_{c}}{\theta}\Big)\Theta(\theta_{g}^{\star}-\theta)\,,

\displaystyle C_{2}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})\big|_{\rm LL}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-\mathcal{R}(\theta_{c}^{2})}}{C_{0}^{q}(m_{J}^{2})}\int_{\theta_{c}}^{R}\frac{d\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}}\>\frac{2}{\theta}\frac{C_{F}}{\pi}\alpha_{s}\bigg(Q_{\rm cut}\Big(\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)^{1+\beta}\bigg)\frac{Q}{Q_{\rm cut}}\Big(\frac{\theta}{2}\Big)^{-\beta}\delta\left(1-\Big(\frac{\theta}{\theta_{g}^{\star}}\Big)^{2+\beta}\right)\,,

where we have expressed the results in terms of the angular bounds
\theta_{c}
and
\theta_{g}^{\star}
defined in Eq. (
12
).

To establish a connection with our results, we must compare with the large
R_{g}
regime of Sec.
3.1
, since due to the associated geometry for power corrections in the SDOE region of the single differential jet mass distribution, this is the primary regime that is relevant for defining the coefficients
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
.
7
7

7

We note that the coherent branching description is based on ordering in a single variable, the jet mass contribution of each emission,
\rho_{i}=z_{i}\theta_{i}^{2}
.
This is in correspondence with the mode picture in the large
R_{g}
regime where the measurement of jet mass provides a distinction between the various EFT modes (see Fig.
2
, left). This is, however, not the case with small and intermediate
R_{g}
regimes:
in the case of small
R_{g}
regime, the primary measurement is the groomed jet radius which distinguishes the modes based on their angular separation. In the intermediate
R_{g}
regime an analogous treatment in coherent branching would presumably involve ordering in two different variables.
Thus, the EFT prediction for the LL double differential cross section in this regime is obtained from the cumulant in Eq. (
86
),

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}\hat{\sigma}_{\rm LL}^{q}}{dm_{J}^{2}d\theta_{g}}\bigg|_{R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dR_{g}}\bigg[\frac{d\sigma^{q}_{\rm LL}\big[\partial_{\Omega}\big]}{dm_{J}^{2}}\bigg(\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}}{\Gamma(-\Omega)}+{\cal Q}^{q}\Big[\Omega,\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}},\beta\Big]\bigg)\bigg]_{R_{g}=\theta_{g}}

\displaystyle=e^{-\mathcal{R}_{q}}\frac{d}{dR_{g}}{\cal Q}^{q}\Big[\Omega,\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}},\beta\Big]\bigg|_{R_{g}=\theta_{g}}\,.

(127)

In the second line we have used that the evolution in this regime is independent of the groomed jet radius and identified the LO differential operator with the exponential of the radiator
\mathcal{R}^{q}
. This follows by setting the Laplace space fixed order ingredients in Eq. (
4.3
) to unity.

We now notice that the fixed-order corrections in Eqs. (
126
) and (
124
) are described in the coherent branching formalism by the single splitting function
p_{gq}
, in contrast with the convolution of the jet and collinear-soft functions in Eq. (
32
). This implies that the coherent branching result ignores some subleading terms in the kernel
\mathcal{Q}^{q}
, which are induced by performing the RG evolution between
\mu_{J}
and
\mu_{cs_{m}}
in Laplace space rather than distribution space. These terms are instead included in our EFT approach; we can remove them by setting
\Omega\to 0
in Eq. (
249
), obtaining

\displaystyle{\cal Q}^{q}\Big[\Omega\to 0,\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}},\beta\Big]=\Theta(\theta_{g}^{\star}-\theta_{g})\,\frac{2\alpha_{s}(\mu_{cs})C_{F}}{\pi}\,\textrm{ln}\Big(\frac{\theta_{g}^{\star}}{R_{g}}\Big)\,,

(128)

where we have used that under the integral

\displaystyle x^{-1-\Omega}=-\frac{1}{\Omega}\delta(x)+\mathcal{O}(\Omega^{0})\,.

(129)

Next, we adopt the natural scale choice for
\mu_{cs}
in Eq. (
90
), noting that

\displaystyle m_{J}^{2}\frac{\theta_{c}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}}=\mu_{cs}^{\rm can.}\,.

(130)

This corresponds to setting
\theta=\theta_{g}^{\star}
in Eq. (
126
), with the difference between the two expressions being beyond LL accuracy.
Substituting Eq. (
128
) in Eq. (
4.5
) gives the integrand of Eq. (
126
). The LL coherent branching result for
C_{1}
then trivially follows by including the additional factor
\theta/2
in the angular integration.

To obtain the LL coherent branching result for
C_{2}
from the EFT formalism we can follow the same steps. We first notice that the anomalous evolution induced by boundary corrections at
\beta=0
affects the cross section beyond LL, and we can thus ignore this correction. The relevant cumulant cross section for large
R_{g}
is then given by Eq. (
120
). Differentiating in
R_{g}
, we get up to
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})
corrections

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{\sigma}_{\rm LL}^{q}(\varepsilon)}{dm_{J}^{2}d\theta_{g}}\bigg|_{R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}}

\displaystyle=e^{-\mathcal{R}_{q}}\frac{d}{dR_{g}}\bigg[\Big(\frac{R_{g}}{2}\Big)^{-\beta}{\cal Q}_{\varepsilon}^{q}\Big(\Omega,\frac{R_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}},\beta\Big)\bigg]_{R_{g}=\theta_{g}}\,

(131)

\displaystyle=e^{-\mathcal{R}_{q}}\frac{Q\varepsilon}{Q_{\rm cut}}\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu_{cs})C_{F}}{\pi}\Big(\frac{\theta_{g}}{2}\Big)^{-(1+\beta)}\frac{e^{\gamma_{E}\Omega}}{\Gamma(-\Omega)}{\cal L}_{0}^{-\Omega}\bigg(1-\Big(\frac{\theta_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}(m_{J}^{2})}\Big)^{2+\beta}\bigg)\,.

The first line is obtained from the same manipulations as in Eq. (
4.5
), while the explicit expression in the second line follows from differentiating the kernel
\mathcal{Q}^{q}_{\varepsilon}
given in Eq. (
119
), which reduces to Eq. (
B.3.2
) when discarding NLO corrections.
In order to compare with the coherent branching result, we again expand out this result in the limit
\Omega\rightarrow 0
. Using the expansion in Eq. (
129
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Gamma(-\Omega)}{\cal L}_{0}^{-\Omega}\bigg(1-\Big(\frac{\theta_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}}\Big)^{2+\beta}\bigg)\,\rightarrow\,\delta\left(1-\Big(\frac{\theta_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}}\Big)^{2+\beta}\right)

(132)

so that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\varepsilon}\bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}\frac{d\hat{\sigma}_{\rm LL}^{q}(\varepsilon)}{dm_{J}^{2}d\theta_{g}}\bigg|_{R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}}\,\rightarrow\,\frac{Q}{Q_{\rm cut}}\frac{\alpha_{s}(\mu_{cs})C_{F}}{\pi}\Big(\frac{\theta_{g}}{2}\Big)^{-(1+\beta)}\delta\left(1-\Big(\frac{\theta_{g}}{\theta_{g}^{\star}(m_{J}^{2})}\Big)^{2+\beta}\right)\,.

(133)

Including the factor of
m_{J}^{2}/Q^{2}
, according to our definition of
C_{2}^{\kappa}
from Eq. (
5
), this yields the LL coherent branching result of Eq. (
126
). Finally, setting
\theta=\theta_{g}^{\star}
using the
\delta
function and noting that

\displaystyle Q_{\rm cut}\Big(\frac{\theta_{g}^{\star}}{2}\Big)^{1+\beta}=\mu_{cs}^{\rm can.}\,,

(134)

we find that the arguments of the running coupling in the two expressions also match.

5
Matching the three regimes

Here we describe our strategy for consistently matching the results in various regimes so as to obtain a smooth interpolation across the entire phase space of
m_{J}^{2}
-
R_{g}
measurement.
Which of the three EFTs come into play will crucially depend on the range over which the groomed jet radius varies for given values of
z_{\rm cut}
,
\beta
and the jet mass.
In the following we first describe the basic strategy for implementation of the matched cross section in Sec.
5.1

using profile functions for scale choices
Ligeti:2008ac
;
Abbate:2010xh
and weight functions which control the contribution from different EFTs
Lustermans:2019plv
.
In Sec.
5.2
we generalize the weight function construction to depend on two variables that track where we are in our two dimensional phase space so that the appropriate factorized formula can be used.
Next in Sec.
5.3
we describe the profile functions for each of the large, intermediate, and small
R_{g}
regimes, and then in Sec.
5.4
how we vary them to obtain an estimate for the perturbative uncertainty.

5.1
Implementation of matched cross section

Here we describe our implementation of the complete cumulative-
R_{g}
and differential jet mass soft drop cross section. This will then set up the groundwork for evaluation of the moments
C_{1,2}^{\kappa}(m_{J}^{2})
.
An important ingredient are the profile functions for our different EFT regions, which are the functions used for the renormalization
\mu_{i}
scales in the factorization formulae, and which depend on both
m_{J}
and
R_{g}
.
First we recall the set of scales that are relevant for the various kinematic regions:

\displaystyle\text{Large $R_{g}$ region}:

\displaystyle R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}(m_{J}^{2},Q_{\rm cut},\beta)<R

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm large}\equiv\{\mu_{J},\mu_{gs},\mu_{cs}\}\,,

\displaystyle\text{Intermediate $R_{g}$ region}:

\displaystyle\theta_{c}(m_{J}^{2})\ll R_{g}\ll\theta_{g}^{\star}(m_{J}^{2},Q_{\rm cut},\beta)

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm int}\equiv\{\mu_{J},\mu_{gs},\mu_{cs_{m}},\mu_{cs_{g}}\}\,,

\displaystyle\text{Small $R_{g}$ region}:

\displaystyle\theta_{c}(m_{J}^{2})\lesssim R_{g}

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm small}\equiv\{\mu_{c},\mu_{gs},\mu_{cs_{g}}\}\,,

\displaystyle\text{Plain jet mass region}:

\displaystyle R_{g}\lesssim R

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm plain}\equiv\{\mu_{J},\mu_{s}\}\,.

(135)

In this analysis we will focus exclusively on the groomed region described by our EFT, although we will also extrapolate our results into the ungroomed region. In the groomed region, we construct the interpolation between different EFT regimes via the following formula:

\displaystyle d\Sigma_{\rm matched}\equiv d\Sigma_{\rm int}|_{\mu_{\rm hyb}}+\big(d\Sigma_{\rm large}-d\Sigma_{\rm int}\big)|_{\mu_{\rm large}}+\big(d\Sigma_{\rm small}-d\Sigma_{\rm int}\big)|_{\mu_{\rm small}}\,,

(136)

where
d\Sigma_{i}(m_{J}^{2})
for
i=
{large, int, small} stand for the cumulative-
R_{g}
factorized cross section formulae in the large, intermediate and small
R_{g}
regimes respectively. We have also indicated the choice of profile scales used for jet, global-soft and collinear-soft scales
used in these factorization formulae utilizing the notation introduced in Eq. (
5.1
).
In Eq. (
136
) the cross sections in the large and small
R_{g}
regimes are evaluated with their respective profile scales, whereas the intermediate
R_{g}
uses either the profile scales in Eq. (
5.1
) (in the two subtraction terms), or a set of hybrid profiles denoted by
\mu_{\rm hyb}
. The hybrid
\mu_{\rm hyb}
profiles are designed such that they interpolate between the
\mu_{\rm small}
,
\mu_{\rm int}
and
\mu_{\rm large}
sets as follows:

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm hyb}\equiv\big(\mu_{\rm small}\big)^{w_{\rm small}}\,\big(\mu_{\rm int}\big)^{w_{\rm int}}\big(\mu_{\rm large}\big)^{w_{\rm large}}\,,\qquad w_{\rm small}+w_{\rm int}+w_{\rm large}=1\,,

(137)

where the exponents
0\leq w_{i}\equiv w_{i}(m_{J},R_{g})\leq 1
should turn on/off in the appropriate regions and add up to one
Lustermans:2019plv
. We give an explicit construction of these weight functions below for our case.

For now, we note that in the large
R_{g}
region we will have
w_{\rm large}=1
(and hence
w_{\rm small}=w_{\rm int}=0
) such that the
\mu_{\rm hyb}
profiles are the same as the set
\mu_{\rm large}
. The term
d\Sigma_{\rm int}|_{\mu_{\rm large}}
in Eq. (
136
) corresponds to evaluating the intermediate
R_{g}
cross section with large
R_{g}
profile scale settings, i.e. by setting
\mu_{cs_{g}},\mu_{cs_{m}}\rightarrow\mu_{cs}
. This leads to exact cancellation of the terms
d\Sigma_{\rm int}|_{\mu_{\rm hyb}}
and
d\Sigma_{\rm int}|_{\mu_{\rm large}}
in the
R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}
region where
w_{\rm large}=1
, and hence the matched cross section is given by

\displaystyle d\Sigma_{\rm matched}=d\Sigma_{\rm large}|_{\mu_{\rm large}}+\big(d\Sigma_{\rm small}-d\Sigma_{\rm int}\big)|_{\mu_{\rm small}}\,,\qquad R_{g}\lesssim\theta_{g}^{\star}\,,\quad(w_{\rm large}=1)\,.

(138)

The term
d\Sigma_{\rm int}|_{\mu_{\rm small}}
here denotes the intermediate
R_{g}
cross section evaluated with the
\mu_{\rm small}
profiles, i.e. by replacing
\mu_{cs_{m}},\mu_{J}\rightarrow\mu_{c}
. Thus the difference
\big(d\Sigma_{\rm small}-d\Sigma_{\rm int}\big)|_{\mu_{\rm small}}

supplements Eq. (
138
) with the power corrections that are missing in both the intermediate and large
R_{g}
factorizations in the
\theta_{c}\lesssim R_{g}
region.

Similarly, in the small
R_{g}
region we will have
w_{\rm small}=1
(and thus
w_{\rm large}=w_{\rm int}=0
). As a result, the
\mu_{\rm hyb}
profiles will coincide with
\mu_{\rm small}
and the terms
d\Sigma_{\rm int}|_{\mu_{\rm hyb}}
and
d\Sigma_{\rm int}|_{\mu_{\rm small}}

in Eq. (
136
) will cancel exactly. Hence, the
d\Sigma_{\rm matched}
in this region will be given by

\displaystyle d\Sigma_{\rm matched}=d\Sigma_{\rm small}|_{\mu_{\rm small}}+\big(d\Sigma_{\rm large}-d\Sigma_{\rm int}\big)|_{\mu_{\rm large}}\,,\qquad\theta_{c}\lesssim R_{g}\,,\qquad(w_{\rm small}=1)\,.

(139)

By using
\mu_{\rm large}
profiles in the intermediate
R_{g}
cross section we turn off the additional resummation relative to the large
R_{g}
regime, so that the second term
\big(d\Sigma_{\rm large}-d\Sigma_{\rm int}\big)|_{\mu_{\rm large}}
captures the power corrections that are missing in the intermediate and small
R_{g}
